## Observations on a certain theorem of Fermat and on others concerning prime numbers by Leonhard Euler

↑ **Parent:** [Mathematics bibliography](mathematics-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Jordan Bell](mathematics-author.md#work-by-jordan-bell), [Work by Leonhard Euler](mathematics.md#work-by-leonhard-euler)

By [Leonhard Euler](mathematics.md#leonhard-euler); English translation by [Jordan Bell](mathematics-author.md#jordan-bell). Presented to the St Petersburg Academy on 26 September 1732; the original Latin paper, "Observationes de theoremate quodam Fermatiano aliisque ad numeros primos spectantibus", appeared in Commentarii academiae scientiarum Petropolitanae 6 (1738), pp. 103–107. The publication note on page one of the translation distinguishes presentation from publication. The linked translation is version three, dated 15 April 2008.

English translation: [https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0501118](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0501118)

Catalogue record: [https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0501118](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0501118)

Cited passages: pp. 1–2, Euler's account of Fermat's proposed prime pattern and the factor $641$ of $2^{32}+1$; p. 1 publication and translator note. The short modular check in [Fermat number](number-theory.md#fermat-number) is presented as a modern verification, not a historical reconstruction of Euler's search.

## Primality testing: then and now by Carl Pomerance

↑ **Parent:** [Mathematics bibliography](mathematics-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Carl Pomerance](mathematics-author.md#work-by-carl-pomerance)

By [Carl Pomerance](mathematics-author.md#carl-pomerance). Slides for the ICERM meeting "Seventy-five years of Mathematics of Computation", 1–3 November 2018.

Online text: [https://math.dartmouth.edu/~carlp/mathcomptalk2.pdf](https://math.dartmouth.edu/~carlp/mathcomptalk2.pdf)

Cited passages: slide 14 states [Pépin's test](prime-number.md#pepin-s-test) and outlines its proof; slides 15–16 describe [Fermat numbers](number-theory.md#fermat-number) and their role in [integer factorization](number-theory.md#integer-factorization); slide 18 gives the [Gauss-Wantzel theorem](straightedge-and-compass-construction.md#gauss-wantzel-theorem) and distinguishes Gauss's construction result from Wantzel's necessity proof. Numerical records elsewhere in these 2018 slides are historical records, not claims about current records.

Our finite examples and images are independently computed.

<h2 id="sur-la-multiplication-de-series-absolument-convergentes-par-des-series-sommables-par-la-methode-de-cesaro-by-a-f-andersen">Sur la multiplication de séries absolument convergentes par des séries sommables par la méthode de Cesàro by A. F. Andersen</h2>

↑ **Parent:** [Mathematics bibliography](mathematics-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by A. F. Andersen](mathematics-author.md#work-by-a-f-andersen)

By [A. F. Andersen](mathematics-author.md#a-f-andersen). Published in Mathematisk-fysiske Meddelelser, volume I, number 4, Det Kgl. Danske Videnskabernes Selskab. The scanned title page names the author and gives Copenhagen and 1918.

Online text: [https://gymarkiv.sdu.dk/MFM/kdvs/mfm%201-9/mfm-1-4.pdf](https://gymarkiv.sdu.dk/MFM/kdvs/mfm%201-9/mfm-1-4.pdf)

Cited passages: §1, printed p. 3, introduces orders of [Cesàro summation](sequence-and-series.md#cesaro-summation) and cites [Ernesto Cesàro](mathematics.md#ernesto-cesaro)'s 1890 publication in Bulletin des Sciences Mathématiques, series 2, volume 14, p. 114; §2, pp. 6–7, discusses the multiplication theorem and its role in developing summability theory. The original 1890 paper was not inspected here; the historical citation refers to Andersen's account.

Our rational-function and sparse-sampling examples and images are independently calculated.

Askey](mathematics-author.md#work-by-richard-a-askey), [Work by Roderick S. C. Wong](mathematics-author.md#work-by-roderick-s-c-wong), [Work by William P. Reinhardt](mathematics-author.md#work-by-william-p-reinhardt)

Chapter authors: [Ranjan Roy](mathematics-author.md#ranjan-roy), [Frank W. J. Olver](mathematics.md#frank-w-j-olver), [Richard A. Askey](mathematics-author.md#richard-a-askey), [Roderick S. C. Wong](mathematics-author.md#roderick-s-c-wong), [William P. Reinhardt](mathematics-author.md#william-p-reinhardt). Their names are given on the official chapter landing page.

Online text: [https://dlmf.nist.gov/1](https://dlmf.nist.gov/1)

Cited passages: §1.10(iv), equation 1.10.9, states the [argument principle](complex-analysis.md#argument-principle); equation 1.10.10 gives the multiplicity-weighted sum of zero locations minus pole locations used in the [weighted argument principle](complex-analysis.md#weighted-argument-principle).

Cited passage: §1.11(ii), equation 1.11.10, gives the coefficient relations used in [elementary symmetric polynomial](polynomial.md#elementary-symmetric-polynomial).

Additional cited passages: §1.13(i), equations 1.13.1–1.13.6, common equation, Wronskian, Abel identity and fundamental-pair equivalence; §1.13(iii), equations 1.13.8–1.13.10, normalized inhomogeneous equation and variation of parameters. Inspected online release: version 1.2.8, 15 September 2026; this is a reference release date, not the original chapter publication date. The source uses analytic coefficients on a simply connected complex domain.

Additional cited section: [§1.2(ii) Finite Series, equation 1.2.10 and last-term definition](https://dlmf.nist.gov/1.2). The five contributors credited above are Chapter 1 authors, not whole-reference authors. Inspected release: version 1.2.8, 15 September 2026; this is an online reference release date.

Additional cited section: [§1.7(i), equation 1.7.2, and §1.7(ii), equation 1.7.5, including equality conditions](https://dlmf.nist.gov/1.7). The existing five chapter contributors remain the Chapter 1 authors; no new publication or release date is inferred.

Additional cited section: [§1.4(v), Functions of Bounded Variation, equations 1.4.33–1.4.34 and the conditions on the derivative integral](https://dlmf.nist.gov/1.4). The existing five chapter contributors remain the Chapter 1 authors; no new chapter publication date is inferred.

Publisher: Cambridge University Press.

Online text: [https://www.seas.ucla.edu/~vandenbe/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf](https://www.seas.ucla.edu/~vandenbe/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf)

Cited passages: Appendix A.5.2, pp. 646–647: symmetric eigenvalue decomposition and definiteness; §2.2.2, p. 30, equation (2.3): ellipsoids and semi-axis lengths; the book uses the inverse quadratic coefficient matrix; §3.1.4, Example 3.2, p. 71: a quadratic function is strictly convex exactly when its symmetric coefficient matrix is positive definite.

Online text: [https://stanford.edu/~boyd/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf](https://stanford.edu/~boyd/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf)

Cited passages: Appendix A.5.5, printed p. 650: block inversion and minimized quadratic form; §C.4.1, printed pp. 672–675: block elimination, Algorithm C.4, factor-solve interpretation and dense complexity.

Online text: [https://web.stanford.edu/~boyd/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf](https://web.stanford.edu/~boyd/cvxbook/bv_cvxbook.pdf)

The front matter states first publication in 2004 and a seventh printing with corrections in 2009. The title page credits [Stephen Boyd](mathematics-author.md#stephen-boyd) and [Lieven Vandenberghe](mathematics-author.md#lieven-vandenberghe) as whole-book coauthors; no separate appendix byline is supplied. Cambridge University Press publishes the book; the Stanford author website hosts this copy.

Cited passages: Appendix A.1.6, printed pp. 636–637, dual norms.

Additional cited passage: §2.1.4, printed p. 24 (PDF page index 37), [convex combinations](convex-set.md#convex-combination) and the definition and minimality of the [convex hull](convex-set.md#convex-hull). The title-page whole-book credits and the separately recorded 2004 first publication and 2009 corrected seventh printing apply to this copy.

Additional cited passages: §5.6.3, printed pp. 251–253, especially equation (5.58), local sensitivity of the optimal value to equality-constraint perturbations. The sign of the [Lagrange multiplier](mathematical-optimization.md#lagrange-multiplier) depends on the chosen sign for the equality term. The title page and front matter retain the whole-book authorship, 2004 first publication and 2009 corrected seventh printing recorded above.

Additional locators: §§5.1–5.2, pp.215–227.

## Random: Probability, Mathematical Statistics, Stochastic Processes by Kyle Siegrist

↑ **Parent:** [Mathematics bibliography](mathematics-bibliography.md)

By [Kyle Siegrist](mathematics.md#kyle-siegrist).

Online text: [https://www.randomservices.org/random/prob/Events.html](https://www.randomservices.org/random/prob/Events.html)

The linked section discusses events and random variables. Its terminology includes a measurable structure with the sample space; this wiki names the underlying set and its sigma-algebra separately.

Additional section: [Expected Value, 2 Additional Properties](https://www.randomservices.org/random/expect/Properties2.html).

Cited passages: Right Distribution Function; A General Definition.

Additional section: [Conditional Probability](https://www.randomservices.org/random/prob/Conditional.html).

Cited passages: Probability Spaces, §4 Conditional Probability, Definitions and Interpretations, The Basic Definition; Basic Theory, The Multiplication Rule; Conditioning and Bayes’ Theorem, countable partition and weighted-average interpretation. Whole-textbook authorship is credited to [Kyle Siegrist](mathematics.md#kyle-siegrist); no separate section byline or publication or revision date is established. The University of Alabama in Huntsville is an affiliation and project supporter, distinct from authorship; Random Services hosts the text.

Cited section: [Expected Value, §6 Generating Functions, Basic Theory, The Probability Generating Function: definition, power-series expansion and absolute convergence on \[-1,1\]; Inversion formula f(k)=P^(k)(0)/k! and its termwise differentiation proof; Factorial moments and variance under the source’s stronger radius \> 1 hypothesis; Independent-sum identity and expected-product factorization; The Moment Generating Function: M(t)=G(exp(t)) and finite-domain requirement.](https://www.randomservices.org/random/expect/Generating.html)

Cited section: [Definition: sequence of independent identically distributed jumps independent of the Poisson process; Properties: conditional mean/variance proof, generating-function conditioning proof, and PGF version; Compound Poisson Distributions: random count independent of the whole sequence; fixed-count-parameter definition.](https://www.randomservices.org/random/poisson/Compound.html)

<h2 id="la-methode-de-cholesky-by-claude-brezinski">La méthode de Cholesky by Claude Brezinski</h2>

↑ **Parent:** [Mathematics bibliography](mathematics-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Claude Brezinski](mathematics.md#work-by-claude-brezinski)

Historical research article by [Claude Brezinski](mathematics.md#claude-brezinski), published in Revue d’histoire des mathématiques 11 (2005), pp. 205–238. Numdam hosts the original journal article. The printed received date, 4 November 2004, and revised date, 11 May 2005, are distinct from publication.

Original article: [https://www.numdam.org/item/RHM_2005__11_2_205_0.pdf](https://www.numdam.org/item/RHM_2005__11_2_205_0.pdf)

Section 4 reproduces a primary manuscript authored by [André-Louis Cholesky](mathematics.md#andre-louis-cholesky) and dated Vincennes, 2 December 1910. Cholesky is the author of that manuscript; Brezinski is the author of the surrounding 2005 article. The manuscript date does not establish an exact invention date or a broad priority claim.

Cited passages: pp. 210–211: constrained geodetic corrections and the matrix $MM^T$; p. 217: manuscript title, 2 December 1910 date and uncertainty about the exact invention date; §4, p. 229: the primary manuscript’s reported 56-equation altitude adjustment for Algerian triangulation and closing date. The primary manuscript transcription was inspected as text; no inspection of handwriting images is claimed.

<h2 id="power-laws-pareto-distributions-and-zipf-s-law-by-mark-newman">Power laws, Pareto distributions and Zipf's law by Mark Newman</h2>

↑ **Parent:** [Mathematics bibliography](mathematics-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by Mark Newman](physicist.md#work-by-mark-newman)

By [Mark Newman](physicist.md#mark-newman), credited as M. E. J. Newman. Published in Contemporary Physics 46, 323–351 (2005). The inspected arXiv version 3 was revised 29 May 2006; version 1 was submitted 1 December 2004. These preprint dates are distinct from the journal publication year.

Preprint: [https://arxiv.org/pdf/cond-mat/0412004](https://arxiv.org/pdf/cond-mat/0412004)

Cited passages: §II, pp. 4–5, equations (3)–(5), density and survival exponents and fitting caveat; §III.D, pp. 10–11, equations (24)–(28), fraction of a total held by an upper population fraction. Its density exponent is one greater than the positive survival exponent used for the [Pareto distribution](continuous-probability-distribution.md#pareto-distribution).

## The Collected Mathematical Papers of James Joseph Sylvester, Volume I

↑ **Parent:** [Mathematics bibliography](mathematics-bibliography.md)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by James Joseph Sylvester](mathematics.md#work-by-james-joseph-sylvester)

Collected papers by [James Joseph Sylvester](mathematics.md#james-joseph-sylvester), edited by [Henry Frederick Baker](mathematics.md#henry-frederick-baker), credited as H. F. Baker in the prefatory note dated April 1904. Cambridge University Press published Volume I in 1904. The collection date is distinct from the original dates of its individual papers. Internet Archive hosts the inspected digitized volume and OCR text.

Digitized volume: [https://archive.org/details/collectedmathem01sylvrich](https://archive.org/details/collectedmathem01sylvrich)

Inspected OCR: [https://archive.org/stream/collectedmathem01sylvrich/collectedmathem01sylvrich_djvu.txt](https://archive.org/stream/collectedmathem01sylvrich/collectedmathem01sylvrich_djvu.txt)

<h3 id="the-collected-mathematical-papers-of-james-joseph-sylvester-volume-i/a-method-of-determining-by-mere-inspection-the-derivatives-from-two-equations-of-any-degree">A method of determining by mere inspection the derivatives from two equations of any degree</h3>

↑ **Parent:** [The Collected Mathematical Papers of James Joseph Sylvester, Volume I](#the-collected-mathematical-papers-of-james-joseph-sylvester-volume-i)  
🏷️ **Tags:** [Work by James Joseph Sylvester](mathematics.md#work-by-james-joseph-sylvester)

Paper 9 by [James Joseph Sylvester](mathematics.md#james-joseph-sylvester), reprinted on pp. 54–57 in [The Collected Mathematical Papers of James Joseph Sylvester, Volume I](#the-collected-mathematical-papers-of-james-joseph-sylvester-volume-i). The reprint identifies the original publication as Philosophical Magazine XVI (1840), 132–135. [Henry Frederick Baker](mathematics.md#henry-frederick-baker) edited the collection; he is not credited as author of this paper.

Inspected collection OCR: [https://archive.org/stream/collectedmathem01sylvrich/collectedmathem01sylvrich_djvu.txt](https://archive.org/stream/collectedmathem01sylvrich/collectedmathem01sylvrich_djvu.txt)

Cited passages: paper 9, printed pp. 54–55, rules 1–4, shifted coefficient rows and the permutation sign sum. The historical word derivatives in the title is not used here to mean differentiation in calculus. The title page and prefatory note establish the separate 1904 collection date.
